--- Strategy Report Received ---
# Scientific Strategy Report: 2D Rayleigh-Taylor Instability (Compressible Euler)

**1. Problem Statement**

A gravitationally unstable stratification of an inviscid ideal gas: heavy fluid ($\rho=2$) below light fluid ($\rho=1$), with gravity acting in $+y$ (i.e., $\nabla\rho \cdot \nabla p < 0$ at the interface → Rayleigh-Taylor unstable). A single-mode vertical velocity perturbation seeds the instability. Expected evolution: exponential linear growth → nonlinear bubble/spike formation → Kelvin-Helmholtz roll-up on the spike shear layers → secondary instabilities and a turbulent mixing zone, with weak acoustic shocks generated by the nonlinear motion. This is a strongly time-dependent, shock/contact-discontinuity-bearing, multi-dimensional problem.

**1. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**

* **Governing Equations:** 2D Compressible Euler with gravitational source, in conservative form:
$$\partial_t \rho + \partial_x(\rho u) + \partial_y(\rho v) = 0$$
$$\partial_t(\rho u) + \partial_x(\rho u^2 + p) + \partial_y(\rho u v) = 0$$
$$\partial_t(\rho v) + \partial_x(\rho u v) + \partial_y(\rho v^2 + p) = \rho g$$
$$\partial_t E + \partial_x\big(u(E+p)\big) + \partial_y\big(v(E+p)\big) = \rho g\, v$$
with equation of state $E = \dfrac{p}{\gamma-1} + \dfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)$ and $g = +1.0$ in the $y$-direction.

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$, $\quad t \in [0, 3.0]$

* **Boundary Conditions:**
  * $x = 0$ and $x = 0.25$: **Periodic**.
  * $y = 0$ and $y = 1$: **Reflective / free-slip walls** (no-penetration $v = 0$; mirror the normal velocity, leave $\rho, u, p$ unchanged in the boundary state).

* **Initial Conditions** (interface at $y = 0.5$):
$$\rho(x,y) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right)$$
$$p(x,y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases}$$
$$u(x,y) = 0$$
$$v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c(x,y) \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin^6(\pi y), \qquad c = \sqrt{\gamma\, p / \rho}$$
(Note: the piecewise pressure is exactly hydrostatic away from the thin interface layer; this is the standard RT initialization and must be implemented verbatim.)

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; gravity vector $\mathbf{g} = [0, +1.0]^T$; $\mu = 0$; thermal conductivity $k = 0$; Atwood number $A = 1/3$; perturbation wavelength $\lambda = 0.25$ (one mode across the domain).

* **Expected Physics:** Linear RT growth (rate $\sigma \approx \sqrt{Agk} \approx 2.9$, nonlinear saturation by $t \approx 1$–$1.5$), rising light-fluid bubbles / falling heavy-fluid spikes, KH roll-up and mushroom structures, transition to a mixing zone centered on $y \approx 0.5$, and weak shocks from wall-reflected acoustics by $t = 3.0$.

**2. Strategic Methodology**

* **Exact solution feasibility: NOT FEASIBLE.** The Method of Characteristics applies only to 1D hyperbolic systems/simple waves; the 2D nonlinear Euler system with a gravitational source and this multi-structure perturbation admits no closed-form solution. The only exact ingredient is the initial hydrostatic equilibrium ($dp/dy = \rho g$), which is already built into the prescribed pressure profile. The subsequent roll-up, mixing, and shock formation are analytically intractable → a numerical method is mandatory.
* **Fourier Spectral: REJECTED.** The domain is non-periodic in $y$ (solid walls), and the solution contains contact discontinuities and shocks → severe Gibbs phenomenon.
* **Steady Continuous Galerkin 2D: REJECTED.** The problem is unsteady with dominant transient dynamics.
* **Selection: DGSEM 2D + time.** High-order accuracy in the smooth stratified regions, local conservation, native support for periodic + reflective boundary states via numerical fluxes, and compatibility with shock capturing and AMR for the discontinuous interface and fine-scale roll-up.

**3. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults.
* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing:**
  * **Variable Selection Analysis:** Refine on **Density $\rho$**.
  * **Justification:** The entire RT dynamics is organized by the density interface — $\rho$ is a sharp, near-binary marker (values $\approx 1$ and $\approx 2$) that stays localized to the interface, shocks, and mixing zone; it never becomes globally noisy. Velocity $(u,v)$ becomes turbulent throughout the mixing region and would trigger refinement everywhere, wasting elements; pressure is smooth except at weak shocks and does not mark the mixing layer. Density is strictly superior for this PDE.
* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments:** None.

**4. Visualization Strategy**

* **Field Selection:** **Density $\rho$** (primary driver — captures bubbles, spikes, and the mixing zone) and **Pressure $p$** (captures shock/acoustic structures). Do not plot $u, v, E$ — they add clutter without diagnostic value here.
* **Task:** Plot $\rho$ (top row) and $p$ (bottom row) at **4 time steps**: $t = 0.75,\ 1.5,\ 2.25,\ 3.0$.
* **Layout:** Grid — Rows = [$\rho$, $p$], Columns = [4 times].
* **Saving Data:** Save the **full solution state** (all conserved variables) at `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth.
* **Constraints:**
  * Save as: `summary_all.png` (strictly).
  * **DISABLE COLORBARS** (`colorbar=false`) if the code is in Julia.
  * Do not use fixed colormaps; let scales adjust automatically.
  * Plot only primary variables; do not compute derived quantities (no vorticity, no Schlieren).

**5. Cross-Validation & Data Integration (Conditional)**

* No external data paths (sensors / reference solutions) are provided in the problem statement → **None**. No interpolation or $L_2$ comparison against external data is required.

**REQUIRED COMPONENTS**

**Main Method:**
* Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time

**Enhancements:**
* Shock Capturing Indicator (density-based)
* Adaptive Mesh Refinement (density-based)

- New Code (lines 1-38):
```julia
# ## Problem Setup: Rayleigh–Taylor Instability
#
# Initializes a heavy fluid below a light fluid, with a smoothed density
# interface at y = 0.5 and the prescribed piecewise hydrostatic pressure.
# A single-mode vertical velocity perturbation seeds the instability.
#
# Gravity acts in the positive y-direction with g = 1.0. The conservative
# source vector is therefore (0, 0, ρg, ρgv).

# %%
@inline function initial_condition_rayleigh_taylor(x, t,
        equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    x_pos, y_pos = x

    rho = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0) * 0.5 *
          (1.0 + tanh(50.0 * (y_pos - 0.5)))

    p = y_pos < 0.5 ? 2.0 * y_pos + 1.0 : y_pos + 1.5

    u = 0.0
    c = sqrt(equations.gamma * p / rho)
    v = -0.025 * c * cos(8.0 * pi * x_pos) * sin(pi * y_pos)^6

    return prim2cons(SVector(rho, u, v, p), equations)
end

@inline function source_terms_gravity(u, x, t,
        equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    g = 1.0
    rho, rho_v1, rho_v2, rho_e = u

    return SVector(0.0, 0.0, g * rho, g * rho_v2)
end

@inline function density_indicator(u, equations)
    return u[1]
end
```

  - [Patcher]: Patch applied successfully.

```julia
# ## Methodology: Forest-of-Octrees (`p4est`)
#
# Defines the Rayleigh–Taylor domain Ω = [0, 0.25] × [0, 1] using an
# adaptive quadtree mesh. The template's single root tree and initial
# refinement level are retained.
#
# Boundary topology:
# * x-direction: periodic, connecting x = 0 and x = 0.25.
# * y-direction: nonperiodic, leaving y = 0 and y = 1 as physical
#   boundaries for the reflective free-slip wall conditions.

# %%
coordinates_min = (0.0, 0.0)
coordinates_max = (0.25, 1.0)

mesh = P4estMesh((1, 1), polydeg=polydeg,
                 coordinates_min=coordinates_min,
                 coordinates_max=coordinates_max,
                 initial_refinement_level=3,
                 periodicity=(true, false))
```

```julia
# ## Boundary Conditions and Hyperbolic System Assembly
#
# The x-boundaries are periodic through the mesh topology. At y = 0 and
# y = 1, `boundary_condition_slip_wall` imposes reflective free-slip walls:
# the normal velocity is mirrored in the boundary state, while density,
# tangential velocity, and pressure are retained.
#
# The pure Euler operator is assembled with the Rayleigh–Taylor initial
# condition and the gravitational source term.

# %%
boundary_conditions = Dict(
    :y_neg => boundary_condition_slip_wall,
    :y_pos => boundary_condition_slip_wall
)

semi = SemidiscretizationHyperbolic(mesh, equations,
                                    initial_condition_rayleigh_taylor, dg;
                                    boundary_conditions=boundary_conditions,
                                    source_terms=source_terms_gravity)
```

- New Code (lines 1-59):
```julia
# ## Strategy: Cross-Language Serialization (.npz)
#
# Saves the complete conservative state (ρ, ρu, ρv, ρE) every 0.1 time
# units. Additional output at t = 0.75 and t = 2.25 ensures that all
# requested visualization times are represented exactly. Pressure is also
# exported for the required density/pressure plots.

# %%
npz_out = joinpath(@__DIR__, "data_npz")
mkpath(npz_out)

dt_save = 0.1
visualization_times = [0.75, 1.5, 2.25, 3.0]
save_times = sort(unique(vcat(collect(0.0:dt_save:3.0),
                              visualization_times)))

function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    # Full conservative state for Ground Truth.
    pd_cons = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi;
                         solution_variables=cons2cons)
    rho = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 1)
    rho_u = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 2)
    rho_v = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 3)
    rho_e = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 4)

    # Pressure is a primary visualization variable in the strategy.
    pd_prim = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi;
                         solution_variables=cons2prim)
    p = getindex.(pd_prim.data, 4)

    filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                        Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))

    npzwrite(filename, Dict("t" => t,
                            "x" => pd_cons.x,
                            "y" => pd_cons.y,
                            "rho" => rho,
                            "rho_u" => rho_u,
                            "rho_v" => rho_v,
                            "rho_e" => rho_e,
                            "p" => p))
    println("Saved: $(basename(filename))")
    return nothing
end

# Preset times force the integrator to stop at each requested output time.
save_npz_callback = PresetTimeCallback(save_times, save_npz_file;
                                       save_positions=(false, false))
```

```julia
# ## Rayleigh–Taylor Density and Pressure Visualization
#
# Creates a 2 × 4 summary figure at t = 0.75, 1.5, 2.25, and 3.0.
# The top row shows density and the bottom row shows pressure. Each panel
# uses automatic color scaling, with no fixed colormap or colorbar.

# %%
println("Generating Rayleigh–Taylor summary plots...")

plot_times = [0.75, 1.5, 2.25, 3.0]
snapshots = []

for t_target in plot_times
    filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                        Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t_target))
    isfile(filename) || error("Missing required snapshot at t = $t_target: $filename")
    push!(snapshots, npzread(filename))
end

style = (
    seriestype = :scatter,
    markersize = 2.5,
    markerstrokewidth = 0,
    aspect_ratio = :equal,
    xlims = (0.0, 0.25),
    ylims = (0.0, 1.0),
    axis = nothing,
    framestyle = :none,
    legend = false,
    colorbar = false,
    margin = 2Plots.mm
)

plots_array = []

# Top row: density at the four requested times.
for (data, t_target) in zip(snapshots, plot_times)
    x = vec(data["x"])
    y = vec(data["y"])

    push!(plots_array, plot(x, y, zcolor=vec(data["rho"]),
                            title="ρ, t=$(t_target)"; style...))
end

# Bottom row: pressure at the same four times.
for (data, t_target) in zip(snapshots, plot_times)
    x = vec(data["x"])
    y = vec(data["y"])

    push!(plots_array, plot(x, y, zcolor=vec(data["p"]),
                            title="p, t=$(t_target)"; style...))
end

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(2000, 1200), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))
println("Done.")
```

Inspector summary:
 The code implements the 2D compressible Euler Rayleigh-Taylor problem with a numerical DGSEM method. Physics: gamma=1.4, gravity g=+1.0 with source vector (0,0,rho*g,rho*g*v), initial density from the prescribed tanh profile at y=0.5, piecewise hydrostatic pressure, u=0, and v=-0.025*c*cos(8*pi*x)*sin(pi*y)^6. Domain is [0,0.25]x[0,1] with x-periodic and y free-slip walls. Spatial discretization uses P4estMesh, polydeg=3, HLL surface flux, and HG shock capturing with density indicator. AMR uses IndicatorLohner on density. Time integration is tspan=(0,3) with RDPK3SpFSAL49, adaptive=false, and CFL=0.5 via StepsizeCallback. The required NPZ snapshots and summary plot are implemented.
Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_Periodic_Reflective\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

--- Advisor Markdown Report Received ---
## Section 1: Status Decision

**Status:** `REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT`

The simulation completed successfully and displays the correct qualitative Rayleigh–Taylor dynamics. However, apparent contact-interface dissipation and obscuring plot artifacts prevent certification as high-fidelity PINN ground truth. This is the first trial, so neither the perfect-score nor history-saturation stopping condition applies.

## Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard

- **Score Integrity:** 47/50
- **Score Accuracy:** 18/20
- **Score Details:** 11/15
- **Score Optimality:** 13/15
- **TOTAL SCORE:** **89/100**

**Error-log check:** No `Minimum achieved error:` line or numerical error norm appears in the supplied stdout tail. **Quantitative error is not applicable as no ground truth is available.** No error value should be inferred from this log.

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

- The solver reached \(t=3.0\), generated `summary_all.png`, and completed without any reported crash, `NaN`, or `Inf`. The 31 solution saves are consistent with the requested \(dt_{\text{save}}=0.1\), including the initial state.
- The density evolution is physically consistent with the specified unstable stratification and \(+y\) gravity:
  - At \(t=0.75\), the interface has developed a coherent deformation.
  - At \(t=1.5\), a central heavy-fluid spike penetrates upward into the light fluid, accompanied by downward light-fluid structures.
  - At \(t=2.25\), the spike broadens and develops mushroom/roll-up-like features.
  - At \(t=3.0\), the disturbance reaches the upper wall and produces a complicated, approximately symmetric mixing region.
- The pressure panels retain the expected background trend—higher pressure toward larger \(y\)—with additional acoustic disturbances and late-time wall interaction. Automatic color scaling means colors should not be compared quantitatively between panels.
- There is no strong visual evidence of Gibbs ringing or catastrophic positivity failure. Nevertheless, positivity and the no-penetration condition cannot be certified from density and pressure images alone.
- The density interfaces, particularly around the late-time roll-up and side structures, appear somewhat soft rather than razor-sharp. Some apparent small-scale mixing may therefore be numerical rather than physical.
- Regular horizontal white stripes occur throughout both fields, including outside the instability region. Their regularity and presence in both variables strongly suggest mesh-line/rendering artifacts rather than physical oscillations, but they obscure fine-scale assessment.

### Diagnosis

The primary physical behavior is correct; the main fidelity concern is **numerical dissipation at contact discontinuities**.

The HLL surface flux does not explicitly restore the contact wave and is generally more diffusive than HLLC for material interfaces. In addition, the density-based shock indicator can classify the steep density interface as nonsmooth, activating the hybrid FV contribution with `flux_lax_friedrichs`. That combination can smear precisely the bubble/spike interfaces that density-based AMR is intended to resolve. AMR alone cannot fully compensate for this dissipation.

The overall architecture remains appropriate. An exact global solution is not feasible for this nonlinear, multidimensional Euler problem with gravitational forcing, wall reflections, interface roll-up, and acoustic/shock structures. The Method of Characteristics does not supply a closed-form solution for this system, so no numerical-to-exact architecture override is warranted.

### Targeted Improvements

- **For Integrity:** No solver-stability fix is required. Remove the horizontal mesh/stripe overlay from the visualization and render the figure at higher resolution. Report density and pressure min/max for each saved time so automatic color scaling does not conceal outliers.
- **For Accuracy:** Add quantitative physical diagnostics: total mass conservation, minimum \(\rho\) and \(p\), maximum CFL/dt behavior, and normal velocity at the two walls. A symmetry residual about \(x=0.125\) would also provide a useful early indicator of implementation or AMR asymmetry. Keep the prescribed initial condition verbatim.
- **For Details:**  
  1. For the next run, replace the HLL surface flux with **`flux_hllc`**, while otherwise holding the configuration fixed to isolate its effect on contact sharpness.  
  2. Retain density-based Löhner AMR, but reduce unnecessary FV activation at smooth contacts. Preferably decouple shock detection from AMR by using a pressure-based smoothness indicator for shock capturing; alternatively, cautiously retune the density indicator and reduce `alpha_max` only if the weak acoustic shocks remain stable.  
  3. If smearing persists after these changes, test `polydeg=5` with a reduced CFL of approximately \(0.3\)–\(0.4\), rather than immediately increasing mesh complexity. Do not change polynomial degree and AMR limits simultaneously.
- **For Optimality:** Retain DGSEM, density-based AMR, the Ranocha DG volume flux, and the current time integrator. The principal methodological upgrade is contact-resolving HLLC flux plus more selective shock capturing, not a change of spatial architecture.

--- 1. Calling Strategist Team ---

=== 🧠 Starting Strategy Team (Proposer-Critic Loop) ===

--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 1/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
**1. Problem Statement**
2D inviscid compressible Euler equations with gravitational source, modeling the single-mode Rayleigh–Taylor Instability (RTI). Heavy fluid ($\rho=2$) lies below light fluid ($\rho=1$) with gravity acting in $+y$, an unstable stratification. This is Trial 2: Run 1 (DGSEM + density AMR + HLL) scored 89/100 with correct physics but excessive contact-interface dissipation and visualization artifacts. The Advisor mandates a targeted refinement: contact-resolving flux, decoupled shock capturing, clean rendering, and quantitative diagnostics.

**1. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)**
* **Governing Equations:**
$$\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\begin{pmatrix}\rho\\ \rho u\\ \rho v\\ E\end{pmatrix} + \frac{\partial}{\partial x}\begin{pmatrix}\rho u\\ \rho u^2 + p\\ \rho u v\\ u(E+p)\end{pmatrix} + \frac{\partial}{\partial y}\begin{pmatrix}\rho v\\ \rho u v\\ \rho v^2 + p\\ v(E+p)\end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix}0\\ 0\\ \rho g\\ \rho g v\end{pmatrix}$$
with EOS $p = (\gamma-1)\left(E - \tfrac{1}{2}\rho(u^2+v^2)\right)$, $\gamma = 1.4$.
* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0,\,0.25]\times[0,\,1.0]$, $t \in [0,\,3.0]$.
* **Boundary Conditions:** $x$: Periodic. $y=0$ and $y=1$: Reflective / free-slip walls (no-penetration $v=0$).
* **Initial Conditions** (verbatim, no modification):
$$\rho(x,y,0) = 2.0 + (1.0-2.0)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left[1+\tanh\!\left(50(y-0.5)\right)\right]$$
$$p(x,y,0) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1, & y < 0.5\\ y + 1.5, & y \geq 0.5\end{cases}$$
$$u(x,y,0) = 0$$
$$v(x,y,0) = -0.025\; c(x,y)\,\cos(8\pi x)\,\sin^6(\pi y), \qquad c(x,y)=\sqrt{\frac{\gamma\, p(x,y,0)}{\rho(x,y,0)}}$$
$$E(x,y,0) = \frac{p(x,y,0)}{\gamma-1} + \frac{1}{2}\rho(x,y,0)\,v(x,y,0)^2$$
* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; gravity vector $\mathbf{g}=[0,\,1.0]^T$ (momentum source in $+y$); $\rho_{heavy}=2.0$, $\rho_{light}=1.0$; $\mu = 0$, $k = 0$ (inviscid).
* **Expected Physics:** Interface deformation from $t\approx 0.5$; heavy-fluid spike rises at $x=0.125$ with light-fluid bubbles descending at $x=0,\,0.25$; mushroom roll-up by $t\approx 2$; mixing region interacts with the top wall by $t=3.0$. Weak acoustic shocks and slip-wall reflections. Solution remains symmetric about $x=0.125$.

**2. Strategic Methodology**
* **Exact solution feasible?** **No.** This is a nonlinear, multi-dimensional hyperbolic system with a gravitational source, contact discontinuities, interface roll-up, shocks, and wall reflections. The Method of Characteristics yields no closed-form solution in 2D (characteristics cross; multi-d coupling precludes implicit inversion). The Advisor explicitly confirms no exact-solution override is warranted. No external reference data is provided.
* **Selection:** **DGSEM in 2D plus time.** Justification: hyperbolic conservation law with discontinuities demands conservative discretization with numerical fluxes; high-order DG resolves the smooth roll-up vortices; AMR tracks the evolving interface; slip walls and periodicity are natural. Fourier spectral is rejected (non-periodic in $y$; Gibbs contamination at contacts/shocks). Run 1 validated this architecture (89/100) — the architecture is retained; only the Advisor's targeted corrections are applied.

**3. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)**
* **Configuration:** Use template defaults. Per the Advisor, **hold the Run-1 configuration fixed** (DGSEM, P4estMesh, density-based Löhner AMR, Ranocha DG volume flux, Lax-Friedrichs FV flux, current time integrator, CFL). **Do NOT change polynomial degree, CFL, AMR levels, or the time integrator this run** — the Advisor's conditional `polydeg=5` / CFL 0.3–0.4 test is deferred to a future run *only if* smearing persists.

* **3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection Analysis:**
    * **AMR variable: Density ($\rho$).** Signal-to-noise reasoning: $\rho$ is the material-interface tracer — it remains sharp, bimodal ($1 \leftrightarrow 2$), and well-defined exactly at the bubble/spike fronts of interest. Velocity $(u,v)$ becomes globally turbulent/chaotic in the mixing layer (wasteful near-uniform refinement). Pressure carries a smooth hydrostatic background gradient ($dp/dy = \rho g$, a factor-2 change across the domain) that would dominate any indicator and trigger spurious global refinement, plus acoustic noise. **Density is strictly superior for AMR.**
    * **Shock-capturing variable: Pressure ($p$) — Advisor-mandated decoupling from AMR.** The RTI interface is a *contact discontinuity*, across which pressure is continuous. The Run-1 density-based indicator misclassified the steep contact as nonsmooth, activating the diffusive FV hybrid precisely at the interface and smearing it. A pressure-based indicator activates only at genuine compressive/acoustic shocks (pressure-discontinuous), leaving the contact to the sharp pure-DG scheme. This decouples shock detection from AMR.

* **3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides):**
    1. **Surface flux: `flux_hllc`** (replaces HLL) — restores the contact wave for sharp material interfaces. All else held fixed to isolate its effect.
    2. **Shock-capturing indicator: pressure-based `IndicatorHennemannGassner`** (replaces density-based); retain the hybrid DG-FV volume integral (`flux_ranocha` DG / `flux_lax_friedrichs` FV).
    3. **Visualization cleanup:** remove the horizontal mesh-line/stripe overlay entirely; render `summary_all.png` at higher resolution.
    4. **Diagnostics (print to stdout at every save):** total mass $\int\rho\,dA$ and drift vs. $t=0$; $\min\rho$, $\min p$ (positivity); min/max timestep (CFL behavior); max $|v|$ at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (no-penetration check); symmetry residual $\|q(x,y)-q(0.25-x,y)\|/\|q\|$ about $x=0.125$.
    5. **Initial condition verbatim** — no re-tuning of the prescribed profiles.

**4. Visualization Strategy:**
* **Field Selection:** **Density $\rho$** (primary driver — material interface, bubbles/spikes, mixing) and **Pressure $p$** (hydrostatic trend + acoustic/shock content; Advisor retained these panels). Do not plot $u, v, E$.
* **Layout:** Grid, 2 rows (fields) × 4 columns (times $t = 0.75,\, 1.5,\, 2.25,\, 3.0$).
* **Saving Data:** Save the **full solution state** (all variables) with `dt_save = 0.1` for Ground Truth. Print min/max of $\rho$ and $p$ at each save.
* **Constraints:** Save as `summary_all.png` (strict). **Disable colorbars** (`colorbar=false`, Julia). **No mesh/element-boundary lines** in the plots. Automatic per-panel color scaling; no fixed colormaps. No derived quantities.

**5. Cross-Validation & Data Integration (Conditional)**
* No external data paths (sensors / reference solutions) are provided in [USER_QUESTION]. **None.** Ground truth consists of the saved full solution states; the diagnostics in §3.1 serve as the quantitative fidelity check.

Cell 4: Spatial Discretization & Hybrid Shock Capturing

- New Code (lines 18-39):
```julia
# 3.  **Interface Flux (`surface_flux=flux_hllc`)**:
#     * **Riemann Solver**: Harten-Lax-van Leer-Contact (HLLC).
#     * **Role**: Computes the numerical flux across element interfaces.
#     * **Physics**: Restores the contact wave to reduce numerical dissipation
#       at the material interfaces driving the Rayleigh–Taylor instability.
#
# 4.  **Indicator Function (`IndicatorHennemannGassner`)**:
#     * Dynamically computes the blending coefficient $\alpha \in [0, 1]$ element-wise.
#     * **Mechanism**: Monitors the spectral decay of pressure in the highest
#       polynomial modes. Pressure-based shock detection avoids activating the
#       diffusive FV flux merely because the density contact is steep. Density
#       remains the independent AMR indicator.
#
# %%
polydeg = 3
basis = LobattoLegendreBasis(polydeg)

@inline function pressure_indicator(u, equations::CompressibleEulerEquations2D)
    rho, rho_v1, rho_v2, rho_e = u
    return (equations.gamma - 1) *
           (rho_e - 0.5 * (rho_v1^2 + rho_v2^2) / rho)
end

indicator_sc = IndicatorHennemannGassner(equations, basis,
                                         alpha_max=1.0,
                                         alpha_min=0.001,
                                         alpha_smooth=true,
                                         variable=pressure_indicator)
volume_integral = VolumeIntegralShockCapturingHG(indicator_sc;
                                                 volume_flux_dg=flux_ranocha,
                                                 volume_flux_fv=flux_lax_friedrichs)
dg = DGSEM(polydeg=polydeg, surface_flux=flux_hllc,
           volume_integral=volume_integral)
```

Cell 8: Data I/O & Surrogate Interoperability
- Action: Preserve the full conservative-state NPZ output and the existing save schedule.
- Action: Add quadrature-based total-mass conservation and drift, density/pressure extrema, accepted-step timestep extrema, wall no-penetration checks, and a full-state symmetry residual. The timestep observer only records accepted steps and does not alter time integration.
- New Code (lines 9-50):
```julia
npz_out = joinpath(@__DIR__, "data_npz")
mkpath(npz_out)

dt_save = 0.1
visualization_times = [0.75, 1.5, 2.25, 3.0]
save_times = sort(unique(vcat(collect(0.0:dt_save:3.0),
                              visualization_times)))

# Diagnostic state; these do not modify the solver or its configuration.
rti_initial_mass = Ref{Float64}(NaN)
rti_dt_min = Ref{Float64}(Inf)
rti_dt_max = Ref{Float64}(0.0)

@inline function rti_mass_integrand(u, args...)
    return u[1]
end

function rti_total_mass(u_ode, semi)
    mesh, equations_local, solver, cache =
        Trixi.mesh_equations_solver_cache(semi)
    u = Trixi.wrap_array(u_ode, mesh, equations_local, solver, cache)

    # Use the DG quadrature and mesh metric terms, rather than a nodal sum.
    return Trixi.integrate(rti_mass_integrand, u, mesh, equations_local,
                           solver, cache)
end

function rti_record_dt!(integrator)
    if integrator.t > 0.0
        dt = integrator.t - integrator.tprev
        if !isfinite(dt) || dt <= 0.0
            dt = integrator.dt
        end
        if isfinite(dt) && dt > 0.0
            rti_dt_min[] = min(rti_dt_min[], dt)
            rti_dt_max[] = max(rti_dt_max[], dt)
        end
    end
    return nothing
end

function rti_wall_max_abs_v(y, v, wall_y)
    distances = abs.(y .- wall_y)
    tolerance = 1.0e-10
    mask = distances .<= tolerance

    # Retain a fallback for floating-point variations in boundary coordinates.
    if !any(mask)
        mask = distances .<= minimum(distances) + tolerance
    end
    return maximum(abs.(v[mask]))
end

function rti_symmetry_residual(x, y, state)
    # Pair scattered AMR samples in common bins. The mirror of bin i is
    # nx + 1 - i, corresponding to x -> 0.25 - x about x = 0.125.
    nx, ny = 16, 64
    sums = zeros(Float64, 4, ny, nx)
    counts = zeros(Int, ny, nx)

    for k in eachindex(x)
        if isfinite(x[k]) && isfinite(y[k])
            i = clamp(floor(Int, (x[k] / 0.25) * nx) + 1, 1, nx)
            j = clamp(floor(Int, y[k] * ny) + 1, 1, ny)
            counts[j, i] += 1
            for n in 1:4
                sums[n, j, i] += state[n][k]
            end
        end
    end

    numerator = 0.0
    denominator = 0.0
    for j in 1:ny, i in 1:div(nx, 2)
        i_mirror = nx + 1 - i
        count_left = counts[j, i]
        count_right = counts[j, i_mirror]

        if count_left > 0 && count_right > 0
            for n in 1:4
                q_left = sums[n, j, i] / count_left
                q_right = sums[n, j, i_mirror] / count_right
                numerator += (q_left - q_right)^2
                denominator += 0.5 * (q_left^2 + q_right^2)
            end
        end
    end

    return denominator > 0.0 ? sqrt(numerator / denominator) : NaN
end

function save_npz_file(integrator)
    semi = integrator.p
    t = integrator.t

    # Full conservative state for Ground Truth.
    pd_cons = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi;
                         solution_variables=cons2cons)
    rho = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 1)
    rho_u = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 2)
    rho_v = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 3)
    rho_e = getindex.(pd_cons.data, 4)

    # Pressure is a primary visualization variable in the strategy.
    pd_prim = PlotData2D(integrator.u, semi;
                         solution_variables=cons2prim)
    p = getindex.(pd_prim.data, 4)

    x = vec(pd_cons.x)
    y = vec(pd_cons.y)
    rho_vec = vec(rho)
    rho_u_vec = vec(rho_u)
    rho_v_vec = vec(rho_v)
    rho_e_vec = vec(rho_e)
    v_vec = vec(getindex.(pd_prim.data, 3))

    mass = rti_total_mass(integrator.u, semi)
    if t == 0.0 || !isfinite(rti_initial_mass[])
        rti_initial_mass[] = mass
    end
    mass_drift = mass - rti_initial_mass[]
    relative_mass_drift = mass_drift / rti_initial_mass[]

    rho_min, rho_max = extrema(rho)
    p_min, p_max = extrema(p)
    wall_bottom = rti_wall_max_abs_v(y, v_vec, 0.0)
    wall_top = rti_wall_max_abs_v(y, v_vec, 1.0)
    symmetry = rti_symmetry_residual(
        x, y, (rho_vec, rho_u_vec, rho_v_vec, rho_e_vec))

    dt_last = NaN
    if t > 0.0
        dt_last = t - integrator.tprev
        if !isfinite(dt_last) || dt_last <= 0.0
            dt_last = integrator.dt
        end
    end
    dt_min = isfinite(rti_dt_min[]) ? rti_dt_min[] : NaN
    dt_max = rti_dt_max[] > 0.0 ? rti_dt_max[] : NaN

    filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                        Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t))

    npzwrite(filename, Dict("t" => t,
                            "x" => pd_cons.x,
                            "y" => pd_cons.y,
                            "rho" => rho,
                            "rho_u" => rho_u,
                            "rho_v" => rho_v,
                            "rho_e" => rho_e,
                            "p" => p))

    println("Saved: $(basename(filename))")
    Printf.@printf("Diagnostics t=%.5f: mass=%.16e, mass_drift=%.3e, relative_mass_drift=%.3e\n",
                   t, mass, mass_drift, relative_mass_drift)
    Printf.@printf("  rho_min=%.6e, rho_max=%.6e, p_min=%.6e, p_max=%.6e\n",
                   rho_min, rho_max, p_min, p_max)
    Printf.@printf("  dt_last=%.3e, dt_min=%.3e, dt_max=%.3e, max_abs_v_y0=%.3e, max_abs_v_y1=%.3e, symmetry_residual=%.3e\n",
                   dt_last, dt_min, dt_max, wall_bottom, wall_top, symmetry)
    return nothing
end

# Observe every accepted step for timestep extrema, then save at preset times.
rti_dt_callback = DiscreteCallback((u, t, integrator) -> true,
                                   rti_record_dt!;
                                   save_positions=(false, false))
save_npz_preset_callback = PresetTimeCallback(save_times, save_npz_file;
                                               save_positions=(false, false))
save_npz_callback = CallbackSet(rti_dt_callback, save_npz_preset_callback)
```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 11: Data Regularization (Rasterization) ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
Cell 11: Data Regularization (Rasterization)
- Action: Replace NaN-producing point splatting with averaged binning followed by deterministic gap interpolation. This produces a complete raster without transparent horizontal stripes while leaving the saved solution data unchanged.
- Action: Use an aspect-appropriate, higher-resolution default grid for the \(0.25\times1\) domain.
- New Code (lines 1-39):
```julia
# ## Methodology: Complete Rasterization for Scattered DG Data
#
# Samples are averaged into uniform bins on Ω = [0, 0.25] × [0, 1].
# Empty bins are then filled by separable linear interpolation: first
# vertically within populated columns, then horizontally across any
# remaining gaps. Unlike nearest-neighbor splatting with NaN pixels, this
# produces a complete image without transparent rows or mesh-like stripes.
# The interpolation is visualization-only and does not modify the saved
# full-state Ground Truth data.
# %%
function rasterize_data(x, y, z, nx=256, ny=1024)
    (nx > 0 && ny > 0) || throw(ArgumentError("Raster dimensions must be positive"))
    (length(x) == length(y) == length(z)) ||
        throw(ArgumentError("x, y, and z must have equal lengths"))

    x_min, x_max = 0.0, 0.25
    y_min, y_max = 0.0, 1.0
    dx = (x_max - x_min) / nx
    dy = (y_max - y_min) / ny

    sums = zeros(Float64, ny, nx)
    counts = zeros(Int, ny, nx)

    # Average all finite samples falling into each pixel.
    for k in eachindex(x)
        xk, yk, zk = x[k], y[k], z[k]
        if isfinite(xk) && isfinite(yk) && isfinite(zk) &&
           x_min <= xk <= x_max && y_min <= yk <= y_max
            i = clamp(floor(Int, (xk - x_min) / dx) + 1, 1, nx)
            j = clamp(floor(Int, (yk - y_min) / dy) + 1, 1, ny)
            sums[j, i] += zk
            counts[j, i] += 1
        end
    end

    grid = fill(NaN, ny, nx)
    for j in 1:ny, i in 1:nx
        if counts[j, i] > 0
            grid[j, i] = sums[j, i] / counts[j, i]
        end
    end
    any(isfinite, grid) || error("No finite samples inside the plotting domain")

    # Fill one-dimensional gaps without overwriting measured/bin-averaged
    # pixels. Constant extrapolation is used only at the two endpoints.
    function interpolate_gaps!(values)
        valid = findall(isfinite, values)
        isempty(valid) && return nothing

        first_valid = first(valid)
        last_valid = last(valid)

        if first_valid > 1
            values[1:(first_valid - 1)] .= values[first_valid]
        end
        if last_valid < length(values)
            values[(last_valid + 1):end] .= values[last_valid]
        end

        for n in 1:(length(valid) - 1)
            left = valid[n]
            right = valid[n + 1]
            if right > left + 1
                value_left = values[left]
                value_right = values[right]
                for k in (left + 1):(right - 1)
                    weight = (k - left) / (right - left)
                    values[k] = (1 - weight) * value_left +
                                weight * value_right
                end
            end
        end
        return nothing
    end

    # Vertical pass: populate complete columns where samples exist.
    for i in 1:nx
        interpolate_gaps!(view(grid, :, i))
    end

    # Horizontal pass: fill columns that contained no original samples.
    # At least one populated column guarantees every row can be completed.
    for j in 1:ny
        interpolate_gaps!(view(grid, j, :))
    end

    all(isfinite, grid) || error("Failed to construct a complete raster")
    return grid
end
```

```julia
style = (
    seriestype = :heatmap,
    aspect_ratio = :equal,
    xlims = (0.0, 0.25),
    ylims = (0.0, 1.0),
    yflip = false,
    axis = nothing,
    framestyle = :none,
    legend = false,
    colorbar = false,
    margin = 2Plots.mm
)

plots_array = []

# Top row: density at the four requested times. Each heatmap receives its
# own data range, so color scaling remains automatic and panel-local.
for (data, t_target) in zip(snapshots, plot_times)
    x = vec(data["x"])
    y = vec(data["y"])
    rho_grid = rasterize_data(x, y, vec(data["rho"]))

    x_grid = range(0.0, 0.25, length=size(rho_grid, 2))
    y_grid = range(0.0, 1.0, length=size(rho_grid, 1))

    push!(plots_array,
          heatmap(x_grid, y_grid, rho_grid,
                  title="ρ, t=$(t_target)"; style...))
end

# Bottom row: pressure at the same four times, with no mesh or element
# boundaries and no explicitly prescribed colormap or color limits.
for (data, t_target) in zip(snapshots, plot_times)
    x = vec(data["x"])
    y = vec(data["y"])
    p_grid = rasterize_data(x, y, vec(data["p"]))

    x_grid = range(0.0, 0.25, length=size(p_grid, 2))
    y_grid = range(0.0, 1.0, length=size(p_grid, 1))

    push!(plots_array,
          heatmap(x_grid, y_grid, p_grid,
                  title="p, t=$(t_target)"; style...))
end

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(3200, 2400), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))
println("Done.")
```

    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

* **Execution:** The solver ran through \(t=3.0\), performed AMR and solution output, and successfully saved the summary figure. The tail contains no crash, NaN, or non-finite-value indication.
* **Density evolution:** The top row shows the expected instability sequence:
  * At \(t=0.75\), a centered heavy-fluid protrusion has formed.
  * At \(t=1.5\), it develops into a narrow rising spike.
  * At \(t=2.25\), the spike rolls up into a mushroom structure while light fluid descends on either side.
  * At \(t=3.0\), the roll-up interacts with the upper part of the domain and produces increasingly complex secondary vortices.
  
  This agrees with the prescribed perturbation: upward motion near \(x=0.125\), downward motion near the periodic side boundaries, and approximate left–right symmetry.
* **Pressure:** The bottom row retains the expected predominantly hydrostatic vertical variation, with increased compression near the upper wall at late time. There is no obvious large-scale pressure oscillation or sign of an incorrect gravity direction.
* **Interfaces:** The principal density fronts and mushroom rims are relatively crisp, consistent with the intended HLLC/contact-resolving upgrade. Some late-time fine-scale roll-up remains soft, but there is no clear large-amplitude Gibbs ringing.
* **Remaining artifact:** Narrow, nearly vertical stripes remain visible in both density and pressure, including regions that should still be smooth or nearly one-dimensional. Their wavelength and persistence are inconsistent with the imposed single-mode \(\cos(8\pi x)\) perturbation. Thus, they are unlikely to represent genuine RTI structures.
* **Diagnostics:** Although the implementation reports a diagnostic suite, none of its numerical values appear in the supplied tail. Consequently, mass drift, positivity, timestep range, wall-normal velocity, and symmetry cannot be independently verified from the provided evidence.

### Diagnosis

The principal remaining defect is **visualization striping/aliasing**, most likely caused by element-wise rendering, inter-element gaps, or nonuniform solution sampling. The claimed removal of explicit mesh lines did not eliminate the artifact. A secondary limitation is mild numerical dissipation of the smallest late-time vortical structures.

The numerical architecture itself is appropriate: DGSEM with HLLC, pressure-based shock detection, and separate density-based AMR correctly distinguishes acoustic/compressive shocks from the material contact. There is no evidence justifying a return to HLL or density-based shock capturing. A global analytical solution is not feasible for this nonlinear, multidimensional RTI problem with gravity, roll-up, and wall interactions, so no exact-method override is warranted.

### Targeted Improvements

* **For Integrity:**
  * Solver execution requires no stability fix.
  * Replace the current element-wise plotting path with **uniformly sampled solution arrays rendered as one heatmap/image per panel**, with no element edge strokes or gaps.
  * As a decisive rendering test, sample the initial condition: density and pressure must be independent of \(x\). Any remaining vertical stripes at \(t=0\) are conclusively visualization artifacts.
  * Include a compact final diagnostic summary in stdout so the physical checks are auditable.

* **For Accuracy:**
  * Report the actual values of relative mass drift, \(\min\rho\), \(\min p\), \(dt_{\min}/dt_{\max}\), wall \(|v|\), and the symmetry residual.
  * Verify that mass drift is near solver/AMR conservation tolerance, density and pressure remain positive, and wall-normal velocities are consistent with weak slip-wall enforcement.
  * Keep the initial conditions, gravity source, HLLC flux, and boundary conditions unchanged.

* **For Details:**
  * Fix the renderer before attributing the stripes to numerical oscillations.
  * Since the same polynomial-degree/CFL configuration has now been used twice, do not repeat it unchanged. For the final resolution refinement, use **`polydeg=5` with CFL approximately `0.35`**, while retaining HLLC, the pressure-based shock indicator, density-based AMR, and the current AMR limits.
  * Do **not** add further mesh refinement; the higher polynomial degree should improve smooth vortex resolution more efficiently. Inspect the resulting contacts for ringing before considering any additional stabilization.

* **For Optimality:**
  * **None.** Retain the present DGSEM/HLLC/decoupled-indicator architecture.

--- 1. Calling Strategist Team ---

=== 🧠 Starting Strategy Team (Proposer-Critic Loop) ===

--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 1/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
# Scientific Strategy Report — Rayleigh–Taylor Instability (2D Compressible Euler)

---

## 1. Problem Statement

Solve the 2D inviscid compressible Euler equations with gravitational source for the single-mode Rayleigh–Taylor Instability (RTI): a heavy fluid ($\rho=2$) sits below a light fluid ($\rho=1$) with effective gravity pointing in $+y$, making the configuration buoyantly unstable. A single-mode velocity perturbation seeds the growth of a rising spike and descending bubbles, culminating in mushroom roll-up, secondary Kelvin–Helmholtz vortices, and interaction with the upper wall by $t=3.0$.

This is **Run 3**. The DGSEM/HLLC/decoupled-indicator architecture (Run 2, score 93) is physically correct and is retained. The remaining defects are (a) a **visualization striping artifact** from element-wise rendering and (b) insufficient resolution of the smallest late-time vortices. The Advisor mandates a renderer fix plus a polynomial-degree/CFL refinement — no architectural changes.

---

## 1. Mathematical Specification (Code-Ready Extraction)

* **Governing Equations:** 2D compressible Euler with gravity source ($\gamma = 1.4$):

$$
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\begin{pmatrix}\rho\\ \rho u\\ \rho v\\ E\end{pmatrix}
+ \frac{\partial}{\partial x}\begin{pmatrix}\rho u\\ \rho u^2 + p\\ \rho u v\\ u(E+p)\end{pmatrix}
+ \frac{\partial}{\partial y}\begin{pmatrix}\rho v\\ \rho u v\\ \rho v^2 + p\\ v(E+p)\end{pmatrix}
= \begin{pmatrix}0\\ 0\\ \rho g\\ \rho g\, v\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
E = \frac{p}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\rho\left(u^2 + v^2\right)
$$

* **Domain:** $(x,y) \in [0,\,0.25] \times [0,\,1.0]$, $\quad t \in [0,\,3.0]$
* **Boundary Conditions:**
  * $x$-direction: **Periodic** ($x=0 \leftrightarrow x=0.25$).
  * $y=0$ and $y=1$: **Reflective / free-slip walls** (no-penetration $v=0$; slip-wall boundary condition).
* **Initial Conditions** (interface at $y=0.5$):

$$
\rho(x,y) = 2.0 + (1.0 - 2.0)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50\,(y - 0.5)\right)\right)
$$

$$
p(x,y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases}
\qquad \text{(hydrostatic: } \tfrac{dp}{dy} = \rho g,\ \text{continuous at } y=0.5\text{)}
$$

$$
u(x,y) = 0, \qquad
v(x,y) = -0.025\; c(x,y)\, \cos(8\pi x)\, \sin^6(\pi y), \qquad c = \sqrt{\gamma\, p / \rho}
$$

$$
E(x,y) = \frac{p}{\gamma - 1} + \frac{1}{2}\rho\, v^2
$$

* **Constants & Parameters:** $\gamma = 1.4$; gravity $g = 1.0$ in $+y$ (source $S = (0,\,0,\,\rho g,\,\rho g v)^T$); $\rho_{heavy}=2.0$, $\rho_{light}=1.0$; interface smoothing factor $50$; perturbation amplitude $0.025$; mode $\cos(8\pi x)$; envelope $\sin^6(\pi y)$; $\mu = 0$, $k = 0$.
* **Expected Physics:** Upward heavy-fluid spike at $x=0.125$, downward light-fluid bubbles at periodic edges; spike formation by $t\approx0.75$, narrow rising spike at $t\approx1.5$, mushroom roll-up at $t\approx2.25$, complex secondary vortices interacting with the upper wall at $t=3.0$. Left–right symmetry about $x=0.125$; pressure remains predominantly hydrostatic with late-time compression near $y=1$.

---

## 2. Strategic Methodology

**Can an exact solution be obtained? No.**
* **Method of Characteristics** is inapplicable: the system is nonlinear, two-dimensional, and source-terms-driven. Characteristics from the single-mode perturbation intersect (caustics) within the spike formation, and the late-time mushroom roll-up is inherently multi-valued — no implicit characteristic system can describe the full evolution. No entropy-condition branch selection can rescue a 2D multi-dimensional roll-up.
* **Fourier Spectral** is inapplicable: the $y$-direction has reflective walls (non-periodic), and the solution develops sharp material contacts and vortex sheets — global Gibbs contamination.
* **Separation of variables / Green's functions** fail for the same reasons (nonlinear advection + discontinuous interface evolution).
* The Advisor explicitly confirms: no exact-method override is warranted for this problem.

**Selection: Discontinuous Galerkin Spectral Element Method in 2D plus time (DGSEM).**
This architecture has been validated across two runs (89 → 93) and correctly reproduces the full RTI sequence. The strategy is surgical: retain the architecture, apply the Advisor-mandated resolution refinement (polydeg 3 → 5, CFL 0.5 → 0.35), and eliminate the visualization artifact via uniform-grid sampling. No additional mesh refinement, no additional stabilization.

---

## 3. Implementation Requirements (CRITICAL)

* **Configuration:** Use template defaults, **except** for the Advisor-mandated overrides in §3.1.

### 3.0.1 Adaptive Mesh Refinement & Shock Capturing — Variable Selection Analysis

* **AMR variable: Density ($\rho$).** The structural feature of interest is the **material interface** (heavy/light contact, spike, mushroom rim). Density is sharp exactly at this interface and smooth everywhere else — a high signal-to-noise tracer. **Velocity** is rejected: the late-time Kelvin–Helmholtz roll-up makes the velocity field globally chaotic, which would trigger refinement across the entire domain and waste elements. **Pressure** is rejected for AMR: it is dominated by the smooth hydrostatic stratification $\partial p/\rho = g\,dy$ spanning the whole domain and would not localize the interface. → Density-based Löhner indicator with `ControllerThreeLevel` (base 4 / med 5 / max 6), interval 20, `adapt_initial_condition=true` — **retained unchanged**.
* **Shock-capturing variable: Pressure ($p$).** Compressive features (acoustic waves, wall compression at late time) manifest as pressure jumps, while the material contact is in approximate pressure equilibrium. A pressure-based Hennemann–Gassner indicator therefore captures genuine compression **without** over-diffusing the density contact. The **decoupling** of the two indicators (pressure → shock capturing, density → AMR) is the endorsed architecture and is retained.

### 3.1 Advisor-Mandated Adjustments (Overrides)

These supersede template defaults and the Run 2 configuration:

1. **`polydeg = 5`** (replaces 3) — final resolution refinement; more efficient for smooth vortex cores than added mesh refinement. **Do not increase AMR levels.**
2. **`CFL ≈ 0.35`** (replaces 0.5) — stability margin for the higher polynomial degree.
3. **Renderer replacement (Integrity fix):** Replace the element-wise plotting path with **uniformly sampled solution arrays rendered as one heatmap/image per panel** — no element edge strokes, no inter-element gaps, no per-element sub-rectangles.
4. **Decisive rendering test at $t=0$:** Sample the initial condition through the *same* rendering path. Density and pressure must be **exactly $x$-independent**; print the max deviation over $x$ to stdout. Any vertical stripes at $t=0$ are conclusively renderer artifacts and must be eliminated before attributing stripes to numerics.
5. **Final compact diagnostic summary in stdout** (auditable values, printed at end of run):
   * Relative mass drift $\left|\,M(t)-M(0)\right|/M(0)$ (must be near conservation/AMR tolerance),
   * $\min\rho$ and $\min p$ (positivity),
   * $dt_{\min}/dt_{\max}$ (CFL behavior),
   * Max wall-normal velocity $|v|$ at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (no-penetration),
   * Symmetry residual $\|q - q(0.25-x)\|/\|q\|$ about $x=0.125$.
6. **Retained unchanged (do NOT modify):** HLLC surface flux; pressure-based HG shock capturing (DG volume flux `flux_ranocha`, FV flux `flux_lax_friedrichs`); density-based Löhner AMR with current limits; initial conditions; gravity source $(0,0,\rho g,\rho g v)$; boundary conditions; time integrator `RDPK3SpFSAL49`, `adaptive=false`, `tspan=(0.0, 3.0)`.
7. **No additional stabilization.** Inspect the resulting contacts for ringing; only act if ringing is demonstrated.

---

## 4. Visualization Strategy

* **Field Selection (2 fields):**
  * **Density $\rho$** — the primary driver and tracer of RTI: interface displacement, spike, mushroom, and mixing are all density features.
  * **Pressure $p$** — verifies hydrostatic balance, gravity direction, and late-time wall compression.
  * Velocity and energy are excluded: they add clutter without new physical information, and no derived quantities (e.g., vorticity) are requested.
* **Task:** Plot $\rho$ (top row) and $p$ (bottom row) at **4 time steps: $t = 0.75,\ 1.5,\ 2.25,\ 3.0$**.
* **Layout:** 2 rows × 4 columns grid.
* **Rendering (CRITICAL):** Each panel is a **single heatmap from a uniformly sampled Cartesian array** (interpolate the DG solution onto a uniform grid, or use the solver's uniform-sampling plot data). No mesh lines, no element boundaries, no gaps. Apply the $t=0$ $x$-independence test of §3.1(4) through this exact path.
* **Saving Data:** Save the **full solution state** (all conservative variables) at frequent intervals (`dt_save = 0.1`) for Ground Truth. Plotting selection affects only `summary_all.png`.
* **Constraints:**
  * Save as: `summary_all.png` (strictly).
  * **DISABLE COLORBARS** (`colorbar=false`) — Julia.
  * Automatic per-panel color scaling; no fixed colormaps or manual limits.

Cell 11: Data Regularization / Uniform Cartesian Sampling
- Action: Replace scattered-sample binning and separable gap filling with direct interpolation of the saved DG visualization blocks onto one global Cartesian array. This removes occupancy- and column-filling-induced stripes while leaving the saved Ground Truth data unchanged.
- New Code (lines 1-89):
```julia
# ## Methodology: Uniform Cartesian Sampling of DG Data
#
# Each saved DG visualization block is interpolated directly onto a single
# global Cartesian grid. The resulting arrays contain no empty pixels and
# can be rendered as one heatmap per panel, without element edges or gaps.
# This interpolation is visualization-only and does not modify Ground Truth.

# Return the two surrounding node indices and the linear interpolation weight.
function rti_linear_bracket(nodes, value)
    n = length(nodes)
    n > 0 || throw(ArgumentError("Cannot interpolate an empty node sequence"))
    if n == 1
        return 1, 1, 0.0
    end

    left = clamp(searchsortedlast(nodes, value), 1, n - 1)
    right = left + 1
    spacing = nodes[right] - nodes[left]
    weight = spacing > 0.0 ?
             clamp((value - nodes[left]) / spacing, 0.0, 1.0) : 0.0
    return left, right, weight
end

# PlotData2D normally supplies three-dimensional element blocks. Also accept
# the two common flattened serializations: (n^2, nelements) and
# (n, n*nelements).
function rti_element_blocks(array)
    ndims(array) == 3 && return array
    ndims(array) == 2 ||
        throw(ArgumentError("Expected DG element-block plot data"))

    nrows, ncols = size(array)
    candidates = (polydeg, polydeg + 1, 2 * polydeg,
                  2 * polydeg + 1, 2 * (polydeg + 1))
    for n in candidates
        if n >= 2 && nrows == n * n
            return reshape(array, n, n, ncols)
        elseif n >= 2 && nrows == n && ncols % n == 0
            return reshape(array, n, n, div(ncols, n))
        end
    end

    throw(ArgumentError("Could not recover DG visualization element blocks"))
end

function rti_sample_uniform_grid(data, variable; nx=256, ny=1024)
    (nx > 1 && ny > 1) ||
        throw(ArgumentError("Uniform grid dimensions must exceed one"))

    x_blocks = rti_element_blocks(data["x"])
    y_blocks = rti_element_blocks(data["y"])
    z_blocks = rti_element_blocks(data[variable])
    size(x_blocks) == size(y_blocks) == size(z_blocks) ||
        throw(ArgumentError("Coordinates and solution data must have matching shapes"))

    x_grid = collect(range(0.0, 0.25, length=nx))
    y_grid = collect(range(0.0, 1.0, length=ny))
    grid = fill(NaN, ny, nx)
    filled = falses(ny, nx)

    for element in axes(x_blocks, 3)
        x_block = view(x_blocks, :, :, element)
        y_block = view(y_blocks, :, :, element)
        x_min, x_max = extrema(x_block)
        y_min, y_max = extrema(y_block)

        # Determine which block dimension represents x. P4est RTI elements
        # are axis-aligned, but this also handles transposed plot data.
        x_first = view(x_blocks, :, 1, element)
        x_second = view(x_blocks, 1, :, element)
        first_spread = maximum(x_first) - minimum(x_first)
        second_spread = maximum(x_second) - minimum(x_second)

        if first_spread >= second_spread
            x_nodes = collect(x_first)
            y_nodes = collect(view(y_blocks, 1, :, element))
            z_block = Array(view(z_blocks, :, :, element))
        else
            x_nodes = collect(x_second)
            y_nodes = collect(view(y_blocks, :, 1, element))
            z_block = permutedims(Array(view(z_blocks, :, :, element)))
        end

        # Sort local coordinates and apply the same permutation to the data.
        x_order = sortperm(x_nodes)
        y_order = sortperm(y_nodes)
        x_nodes = x_nodes[x_order]
        y_nodes = y_nodes[y_order]
        z_block = z_block[x_order, y_order]

        # Include boundary pixels despite floating-point coordinate roundoff.
        padding = 1.0e-12
        i_first = max(1, searchsortedfirst(x_grid, x_min - padding))
        i_last = min(nx, searchsortedlast(x_grid, x_max + padding))
        j_first = max(1, searchsortedfirst(y_grid, y_min - padding))
        j_last = min(ny, searchsortedlast(y_grid, y_max + padding))

        for j in j_first:j_last
            y_left, y_right, wy = rti_linear_bracket(y_nodes, y_grid[j])
            for i in i_first:i_last
                filled[j, i] && continue

                x_left, x_right, wx =
                    rti_linear_bracket(x_nodes, x_grid[i])

                z00 = z_block[x_left, y_left]
                z10 = z_block[x_right, y_left]
                z01 = z_block[x_left, y_right]
                z11 = z_block[x_right, y_right]

                lower = (1.0 - wx) * z00 + wx * z10
                upper = (1.0 - wx) * z01 + wx * z11
                grid[j, i] = (1.0 - wy) * lower + wy * upper
                filled[j, i] = true
            end
        end
    end

    missing_count = count(.!filled)
    missing_count == 0 ||
        error("Uniform DG sampling left $missing_count grid points uncovered")
    all(isfinite, grid) ||
        error("Uniform DG sampling produced non-finite values")

    return x_grid, y_grid, grid
end

# Maximum departure from x-independence, measured against each row's mean.
function rti_max_x_deviation(grid)
    maximum_deviation = 0.0
    for j in axes(grid, 1)
        row = view(grid, j, :)
        row_mean = sum(row) / length(row)
        for value in row
            maximum_deviation = max(maximum_deviation,
                                    abs(value - row_mean))
        end
    end
    return maximum_deviation
end
```

```julia
# ## Rayleigh–Taylor Density and Pressure Visualization
#
# Creates a 2 × 4 summary figure at t = 0.75, 1.5, 2.25, and 3.0.
# Every panel is one heatmap of a uniformly sampled Cartesian array.
# The t = 0 renderer test uses this same sampling path.
# %%
println("Generating Rayleigh–Taylor summary plots...")

# Decisive renderer test: the initial density and pressure are x-independent.
initial_filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                            Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", 0.0))
isfile(initial_filename) ||
    error("Missing initial snapshot for renderer test: $initial_filename")
initial_snapshot = npzread(initial_filename)

x_grid_initial, y_grid_initial, rho_grid_initial =
    rti_sample_uniform_grid(initial_snapshot, "rho")
initial_pressure_sample = rti_sample_uniform_grid(initial_snapshot, "p")
p_grid_initial = initial_pressure_sample[3]

rho_x_deviation = rti_max_x_deviation(rho_grid_initial)
p_x_deviation = rti_max_x_deviation(p_grid_initial)
Printf.@printf("Renderer t=0 x-independence: max_density_deviation=%.16e, max_pressure_deviation=%.16e\n",
               rho_x_deviation, p_x_deviation)

plot_times = [0.75, 1.5, 2.25, 3.0]
snapshots = []
for t_target in plot_times
    filename = joinpath(npz_out,
                        Printf.@sprintf("time_%08.5f.npz", t_target))
    isfile(filename) ||
        error("Missing required snapshot at t = $t_target: $filename")
    push!(snapshots, npzread(filename))
end

# Sample each requested snapshot onto the same global Cartesian grid.
sampled_snapshots = []
for data in snapshots
    x_grid, y_grid, rho_grid = rti_sample_uniform_grid(data, "rho")
    pressure_sample = rti_sample_uniform_grid(data, "p")
    p_grid = pressure_sample[3]
    push!(sampled_snapshots,
          (x=x_grid, y=y_grid, rho=rho_grid, p=p_grid))
end

style = (
    seriestype = :heatmap,
    aspect_ratio = :equal,
    xlims = (0.0, 0.25),
    ylims = (0.0, 1.0),
    yflip = false,
    axis = nothing,
    framestyle = :none,
    legend = false,
    colorbar = false,
    margin = 2Plots.mm
)

plots_array = []

# Top row: density. Color scaling remains automatic and panel-local.
for (sampled, t_target) in zip(sampled_snapshots, plot_times)
    push!(plots_array,
          heatmap(sampled.x, sampled.y, sampled.rho,
                  title="ρ, t=$(t_target)"; style...))
end

# Bottom row: pressure, with no mesh lines, element edges, or colorbars.
for (sampled, t_target) in zip(sampled_snapshots, plot_times)
    push!(plots_array,
          heatmap(sampled.x, sampled.y, sampled.p,
                  title="p, t=$(t_target)"; style...))
end

println("Saving summary_all.png...")
final_plot = plot(plots_array..., layout=(2, 4),
                  size=(3200, 2400), margin=5Plots.mm)
savefig(final_plot, joinpath(@__DIR__, "summary_all.png"))

# Print auditable end-of-run values after figure generation so they appear
# in the stdout tail rather than being buried among intermediate saves.
final_diagnostics = rti_final_diagnostics[]
final_diagnostics === nothing &&
    error("Final diagnostics were not recorded")

println("Final diagnostic summary:")
Printf.@printf("  t=%.5f, relative_mass_drift=%.6e, min_rho=%.6e, min_p=%.6e\n",
               final_diagnostics.t,
               final_diagnostics.relative_mass_drift,
               final_diagnostics.rho_min,
               final_diagnostics.p_min)
Printf.@printf("  dt_min=%.6e, dt_max=%.6e, max_abs_v_y0=%.6e, max_abs_v_y1=%.6e, symmetry_residual=%.6e\n",
               final_diagnostics.dt_min,
               final_diagnostics.dt_max,
               final_diagnostics.max_abs_v_y0,
               final_diagnostics.max_abs_v_y1,
               final_diagnostics.symmetry_residual)
println("Done.")
```

--- 4. Calling Debugging Team ---
Inspector summary:
 Implementation is a numerical DGSEM solver for the 2D compressible Euler equations with gamma=1.4 and the prescribed gravity source. Domain is [0,0.25] x [0,1] with t in [0,3], periodic x-boundaries, and slip walls at y=0 and y=1. Initial conditions match the strategy: smoothed tanh density interface at y=0.5, piecewise hydrostatic pressure, u=0, v=-0.025*c*cos(8*pi*x)*sin(pi*y)^6, and total energy through prim2cons. Spatial discretization uses polydeg=5 Lobatto basis, HLLC surface flux, pressure-based Hennemann-Gassner shock capturing with flux_ranocha DG and flux_lax_friedrichs FV, and density-based Lohner AMR with ControllerThreeLevel base=4/med=5/max=6, interval=20, adapt_initial_condition=true. Time integration uses RDPK3SpFSAL49 with adaptive=false, tspan=(0.0,3.0), and CFL=0.35 via StepsizeCallback. Visualization samples the DG solution onto a uniform Cartesian grid and saves summary_all.png as a 2x4 heatmap grid of density and pressure at t=0.75,1.5,2.25,3.0 with colorbar=false. The code also performs the required t=0 renderer x-independence test and prints the final diagnostic summary for mass drift, positivity, dt extrema, wall |v|, and symmetry residual.
Proposed name:CompressibleEuler_RayleighTaylor_Periodic_Reflective\Question :Let's solve the 2D Compressible Euler equations for the Rayleigh-Taylor Instability.

**Physics Parameters:**
* **Equations:** Compressible Euler ($\gamma = 1.4$).
* **Gravity:** $g = 1.0$ acting in the **positive $y$-direction** (The source term adds momentum in $+y$).
* **Inviscid:** $\mu = 0$, thermal conductivity $k = 0$.

**Domain:**
$(x,y) \in [0, 0.25] \times [0, 1.0]$
Time interval: $t \in [0, 3.0]$

**Boundary Conditions:**
* $x$-direction: **Periodic**.
* $y$-direction: **Reflective / Free-slip walls** at $y=0$ and $y=1$ (No-penetration condition $v=0$).

**Initial Conditions:**
The domain is split at the interface $y=0.5$.
* **Density ($\rho$):** A smoothed interface between $\rho_{heavy} = 2.0$ (bottom) and $\rho_{light} = 1.0$ (top).
    $$ \rho(y) = \rho_{heavy} + (\rho_{light} - \rho_{heavy}) \cdot \frac{1}{2}\left(1 + \tanh\left(50(y - 0.5)\right)\right) $$
* **Pressure ($p$):** Hydrostatic balance ($\frac{dp}{dy} = \rho g$).
    $$ p(y) = \begin{cases} 2y + 1 & \text{if } y < 0.5 \\ y + 1.5 & \text{if } y \geq 0.5 \end{cases} $$
* **Velocity:** Initialized with zero horizontal velocity ($u=0$) and a perturbation in vertical velocity ($v$):
    $$ v(x,y) = -0.025 \cdot c \cdot \cos(8\pi x) \cdot \sin(\pi y)^6 $$
    Where $c = \sqrt{\gamma p / \rho}$ is the local speed of sound.

## Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback

### Observation

- **Execution and conservation:** The solver reaches \(t=3.0\), generates `summary_all.png`, and reports finite, positive fields:
  - Relative mass drift: \(4.9155\times10^{-12}\), excellent for an AMR calculation.
  - \(\min\rho=0.35441>0\) and \(\min p=1.20991>0\): positivity is maintained.
  - Maximum wall-normal velocities are approximately \(9.77\times10^{-5}\) at \(y=0\) and \(3.08\times10^{-4}\) at \(y=1\), indicating good no-penetration behavior.
- **Renderer correction:** The previous element-striping artifact is no longer visible. The \(t=0\) test gives maximum \(x\)-deviations of \(1.44\times10^{-14}\) in density and \(1.38\times10^{-8}\) in pressure, confirming effective initial \(x\)-independence through the plotting path.
- **Density evolution:** The panels reproduce the expected RTI sequence:
  - \(t=0.75\): single-mode interface displacement.
  - \(t=1.5\): narrow rising heavy-fluid spike with descending light-fluid regions.
  - \(t=2.25\): mushroom formation and roll-up.
  - \(t=3.0\): secondary Kelvin–Helmholtz structures and interaction with the upper wall.
- **Pressure:** The predominantly hydrostatic vertical stratification is retained, with late-time compression near the upper wall. No obvious nonphysical pressure oscillation is visible.
- **Fine-scale quality:** Contacts and mushroom rims appear sharp, with no clear Gibbs ringing or gross interface smearing. Nevertheless, visual sharpness alone does not establish convergence of the smallest \(t=3\) vortices.
- **Remaining diagnostic concerns:**
  - The **symmetry residual is \(3.4052\times10^{-2}\)**, or approximately **3.4%**. For this formally symmetric problem, that is large enough to investigate, although late-time chaotic dynamics, non-symmetric AMR, or an interpolation/indexing error in the diagnostic could explain it.
  - `dt_min=5.551115e-17` is suspicious. Given the successful completion and otherwise reasonable `dt_max=3.89756e-4`, it is likely a final-step clipping or diagnostic artifact, but this must be verified rather than assumed.

### Diagnosis

This is a **high-quality, stable DGSEM RTI calculation**, with the remaining deficiencies primarily in **verification and diagnostics**, not in the physical architecture. There is no demonstrated need for a more dissipative flux or additional shock capturing; either would risk unnecessarily smearing the material interface.

The exact-method check also favors retaining the current approach. The full nonlinear, two-dimensional compressible Euler system with gravity, wall interaction, and vortex roll-up has no feasible closed-form analytical solution. Linearized Fourier analysis or characteristics could describe only restricted early-time behavior, not the requested evolution through \(t=3\).

### Targeted Improvements

- **For Integrity:**
  - Audit the timestep diagnostic using the raw accepted-step history. Report the minimum over ordinary integration steps separately from any final `tspan`-clipping step and from initialization values.
  - If genuinely tiny steps occur repeatedly, inspect the interaction between AMR reinitialization and `StepsizeCallback`; only then consider reducing CFL to **0.30**. Do not blindly tighten CFL based solely on the current summary.

- **For Accuracy:**
  - Recompute the symmetry residual on a **cell-centered symmetric uniform grid without a duplicated periodic endpoint**, using the exact index pairing \(i\leftrightarrow N_x-1-i\). Report residuals separately for each conservative variable and at several times to identify when asymmetry first grows.
  - If the residual remains significant after correcting the diagnostic, use **mirror-paired AMR refinement/coarsening**, or a symmetric fixed-mesh control run at comparable degrees of freedom, to distinguish AMR-induced symmetry breaking from physical chaotic amplification.

- **For Details:**
  - Perform one non-repeating resolution check with **`polydeg=6`, `CFL=0.30`**, retaining HLLC, the present pressure-based HG indicator, and the current AMR level limits. Compare the density interface and vortex cores at \(t=2.25\) and \(t=3.0\) on an identical sampling grid.
  - Use the higher-degree result as the production configuration only if it materially changes those features. Otherwise, the present `polydeg=5` run is the more cost-effective choice.
  - **Do not** revert to HLL/Lax–Friedrichs surface fluxes or add indiscriminate stabilization; no ringing is demonstrated, and those changes would increase contact dissipation.

- **For Optimality:** None. DGSEM with HLLC, pressure-based shock capturing, density-based AMR, and the current explicit low-storage integrator remains an appropriate architecture for this problem.
